duration-200 md:block pointer-events-none opacity-0"></div><main><div><section class="bg-primary-inverse py-xl relative flex min-h-[100svh] items-center" data-theme="black"><div class="absolute inset-0"><img alt="HarveyLAB" loading="lazy" width="1928" height="1088" decoding="async" data-nimg="1" class="rounded-image size-full object-cover" style="color:transparent" src="https://cdn.sanity.io/images/07s0r5r6/production/5536437af879a7dd7c5cb46bf57597266f44ba38-1928x1088.svg"/><div class="absolute inset-0 bg-[linear-gradient(270deg,transparent_29.31%,#000_100%)]"></div></div><div class="w-full mx-auto px-7 md:px-8 lg:px-9 xl:px-10 max-w-page-width grid grid-cols-2 gap-[14px] md:grid-cols-6 md:gap-4 lg:grid-cols-12 lg:gap-[18px] xl:gap-5"><div class="col-span-2 md:col-span-6 lg:col-start-2 lg:col-end-12 xl:col-span-10 xl:col-start-2 grid grid-cols-subgrid"><div class="gap-md flex flex-col sm:!pr-12 !max-w-post z-1 relative col-span-full md:col-span-4 lg:!col-span-6"><span class="text-primary text-body-2 font-medium">Insights</span><div class="gap-sm flex flex-col"><h1 class="text-primary text-heading-2 text-balance">Open-Sourcing :Harvey:’s Long Horizon Legal Agent Benchmark</h1><p class="text-body-0 text-secondary">An open-source benchmark built to evaluate and improve agent capabilities for supporting legal work.</p></div><div class="text-secondary text-body-2 flex flex-wrap items-center gap-x-1">by<!-- --> <span><a class="underline decoration-solid decoration-auto underline-offset-auto [text-decoration-skip-ink:none] [text-underline-position:from-font] cursor-pointer" href="/en-US/blog/author/niko-grupen">Niko Grupen</a>, </span><span><a class="underline decoration-solid decoration-auto underline-offset-auto [text-decoration-skip-ink:none] [text-underline-position:from-font] cursor-pointer" href="/en-US/blog/author/gabe-pereyra">Gabe Pereyra</a>, and </span><span><a class="underline decoration-solid decoration-auto underline-offset-auto [text-decoration-skip-ink:none] [text-underline-position:from-font] cursor-pointer" href="/en-US/blog/author/julio-pereyra">Julio Pereyra</a></span><span class="mx-2">•</span><span>May 6, 2026</span></div></div></div></div></section></div><div class="bg-primary" data-theme="white"><div class="mx-auto w-full max-w-[1728px]"><div class="mx-7 grid grid-cols-2 gap-[14px] md:mx-8 md:grid-cols-6 md:gap-4 lg:mx-9 lg:grid-cols-12 lg:gap-[18px] xl:mx-10 xl:gap-5 pt-lg pb-xl"><div class="col-span-2 md:col-start-2 md:col-end-6 lg:col-start-3 lg:col-end-11 flex flex-col"><div class="my-sm py-md col-span-full md:col-span-4 lg:col-span-8"><div><figure class="cursor-pointer flex flex-col items-start" tabindex="0" role="button" aria-label="Open Research Partners in full screen"><img alt="Research Partners" loading="lazy" width="1920" height="1080" decoding="async" data-nimg="1" class="rounded-image rounded-image size-full object-cover aspect-auto" style="color:transparent" src="https://cdn.sanity.io/images/07s0r5r6/production/7f2dd853e68c1f9d1600b2e589b5eb3e2135b6ee-1920x1080.svg"/></figure></div></div><p class="w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8">We’re introducing Harvey’s Legal Agent Benchmark (LAB), an open-source benchmark for legal agents. LAB was built to evaluate and improve agent capabilities for supporting real world work done by lawyers. Each task consists of an instruction, a client matter containing relevant materials, and a requirement that the agent produce a work product for review. This structure is designed to mirror how work is assigned, performed, and reviewed at large law firms.</p><p class="w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8">The goal of LAB is to provide a clear picture of how agents can be deployed to support legal work in the real world. By articulating where agents can do all, some, or none of a task, LAB helps law firms measure the ROI of AI investments and where such investments can augment their teams’ work.</p><p class="w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8">The first version of LAB includes more than 1,200 agent tasks across 24 legal practice areas, and is evaluated by over 75,000 expert-written rubric criteria. We are open-sourcing LAB to give model providers, agent builders, researchers, and law firms a shared way to measure progress on long-horizon legal agents.</p><p class="w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8">We&#x27;re intentionally launching LAB without a leaderboard because we expect the dataset to evolve over time and we want to work with the community to ensure results are clear and intuitive in how they convey agent performance. In the coming weeks we will work with research partners to get baseline results on LAB and publish a leaderboard to provide perspective on the current state of legal agents. In addition, we will publish standards for normalizing submissions to allow people to identify improvements and track benchmark progress as the benchmark itself evolves with new tasks, practice areas, and knowledge work disciplines.</p><p class="w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8">You can find the open-source version of LAB <a class="underline cursor-pointer" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank" href="https://github.com/harveyai/harvey-labs">here</a>.</p><h2 class="w-full mx-auto max-w-post mb-sm mt-sm text-body-0 text-primary font-medium mb-sm">Why we built an agent benchmark</h2><p class="w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8">At Harvey, we have spent the past year building agents for the legal domain. This has forced us to think deeply about how to measure their performance on real-world legal tasks.</p><p class="w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8">To date, there has not been a benchmark that illustrates agent progress for long-horizon legal work. Existing evaluations, including <a class="underline cursor-pointer" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank" href="https://hazyresearch.stanford.edu/legalbench/">LegalBench</a>, <a class="underline cursor-pointer" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank" href="https://www.atticusprojectai.org/cuad/">CUAD</a>, <a class="underline cursor-pointer" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank" href="https://lexam-benchmark.github.io/">LEXam</a>, and our own earlier work on <a class="underline cursor-pointer" href="/en-US/blog/introducing-biglaw-bench">BigLaw Bench</a>, have graded short-horizon reasoning: read a contract, answer a question, compare cases, or analyze an argument.</p><p class="w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8">In coding, agent benchmarks have served as an important leading indicator of agent capability. For example, agent scores on <a class="underline cursor-pointer" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank" href="https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.16941">SWE-Bench Pro</a>, <a class="underline cursor-pointer" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank" href="https://www.swebench.com/verified.html">SWE-Bench Verified</a>, and <a class="underline cursor-pointer" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank" href="https://www.tbench.ai/benchmarks/terminal-bench-2">Terminal-Bench 2.0</a> reflected a step-function improvement around the same time our engineering team started to feel the shift in practice; a moment crystallized by Karpathy’s observation that coding agents <a class="underline cursor-pointer" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank" href="https://x.com/karpathy/status/2026731645169185220?s=20">“basically didn’t work before December and basically work since”</a>.</p><div class="my-sm py-md col-span-full md:col-span-4 lg:col-span-8"><div><figure class="cursor-pointer flex flex-col items-start" tabindex="0" role="button" aria-label="Open In coding, agent benchmarks have served as an important leading indicator of agent capability.  in full screen"><img alt="In coding, agent benchmarks have served as an important leading indicator of agent capability. " loading="lazy" width="3840" height="2160" decoding="async" data-nimg="1" class="rounded-image rounded-image size-full object-cover aspect-auto" style="color:transparent" sizes="(max-width: 1024px) 100vw, (max-width: 1728px) 66vw, 1092px" srcSet="/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fcdn.sanity.io%2Fimages%2F07s0r5r6%2Fproduction%2F42a0a9face8ec287a37624df5211f723f041dfc6-3840x2160.png%3Fauto%3Dformat&amp;w=640&amp;q=80 640w, /_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fcdn.sanity.io%2Fimages%2F07s0r5r6%2Fproduction%2F42a0a9face8ec287a37624df5211f723f041dfc6-3840x2160.png%3Fauto%3Dformat&amp;w=750&amp;q=80 750w, /_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fcdn.sanity.io%2Fimages%2F07s0r5r6%2Fproduction%2F42a0a9face8ec287a37624df5211f723f041dfc6-3840x2160.png%3Fauto%3Dformat&amp;w=828&amp;q=80 828w, /_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fcdn.sanity.io%2Fimages%2F07s0r5r6%2Fproduction%2F42a0a9face8ec287a37624df5211f723f041dfc6-3840x2160.png%3Fauto%3Dformat&amp;w=1080&amp;q=80 1080w, /_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fcdn.sanity.io%2Fimages%2F07s0r5r6%2Fproduction%2F42a0a9face8ec287a37624df5211f723f041dfc6-3840x2160.png%3Fauto%3Dformat&amp;w=1200&amp;q=80 1200w, /_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fcdn.sanity.io%2Fimages%2F07s0r5r6%2Fproduction%2F42a0a9face8ec287a37624df5211f723f041dfc6-3840x2160.png%3Fauto%3Dformat&amp;w=1920&amp;q=80 1920w, /_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fcdn.sanity.io%2Fimages%2F07s0r5r6%2Fproduction%2F42a0a9face8ec287a37624df5211f723f041dfc6-3840x2160.png%3Fauto%3Dformat&amp;w=2048&amp;q=80 2048w, /_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fcdn.sanity.io%2Fimages%2F07s0r5r6%2Fproduction%2F42a0a9face8ec287a37624df5211f723f041dfc6-3840x2160.png%3Fauto%3Dformat&amp;w=3840&amp;q=80 3840w" src="/_next/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fcdn.sanity.io%2Fimages%2F07s0r5r6%2Fproduction%2F42a0a9face8ec287a37624df5211f723f041dfc6-3840x2160.png%3Fauto%3Dformat&amp;w=3840&amp;q=80"/><figcaption class="mt-xs text-body-3 text-muted">Agent Inflection Point</figcaption></figure></div></div><p class="w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8">That pattern is now extending beyond coding. Benchmarks such as <a class="underline cursor-pointer" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank" href="https://openai.com/index/gdpval/">GDPval</a>, <a class="underline cursor-pointer" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank" href="https://xlang.ai/blog/osworld-verified">OSWorld-Verified</a>, <a class="underline cursor-pointer" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank" href="https://openai.com/index/browsecomp/">BrowseComp</a>, <a class="underline cursor-pointer" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank" href="https://github.com/scaleapi/mcp-atlas">MCP Atlas</a>, <a class="underline cursor-pointer" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank" href="https://www.vals.ai/benchmarks/finance_agent">FinanceAgent</a>, <a class="underline cursor-pointer" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank" href="https://agi.safe.ai/">Humanity’s Last Exam</a>, and <a class="underline cursor-pointer" rel="noopener noreferrer" target="_blank" href="https://www.mercor.com/apex/apex-agents-leaderboard/">APEX-Agents</a> have helped make progress legible across real-world knowledge work, computer use, web research, tool use, financial analysis, frontier reasoning, and professional-services tasks.</p><p class="w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8">LAB is intended to provide this same legible index to law firms seeking to deploy and maximize ROI from AI systems. Understanding where agents are capable across practice areas allows firms to identify opportunities to deploy them in order to accelerate client value while moving lawyers to a high-leverage delegate and review pattern. Identifying areas where agents struggle also allows firms to understand where task execution should remain heavily human-in-the-loop. Combined, this context allows law firms to make deployment decisions that are both responsible, secure, and maximally effective for their clients.</p><p class="w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8">Initial results on LAB for leading open and closed-source models will be published in the coming weeks following additional input from research partners and feedback from the community. These conversations will ensure that benchmark scores represent agent performance in a clear, unbiased, and transparent manner. This release will also provide guidance for submitting benchmark runs under normalized conditions to allow third-parties to communicate improvements made on any part of the agent stack and to track how scores change as the benchmark evolves with new tasks and other extensions.</p><p class="w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8">A strong public benchmark doesn’t only measure progress, it helps accelerate it. We are already seeing LAB being used by both our internal teams for product evaluation and the larger research community to explore open-weight post-training, auto-research, memory, domain-specific legal skills, and harness optimizations for long-horizon agent work. We’re excited for the potential of LAB to create a shared foundation for progress on legal agents, and to help bridge the AI research and legal communities.</p><h2 class="w-full mx-auto max-w-post mb-sm mt-sm text-body-0 text-primary font-medium mb-sm">Legal Agent Benchmark</h2><p class="w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8">LAB is a client matter-centric benchmark. It is built to mirror how legal work is actually delivered at a law firm, and we’ve mapped each step of the agent execution and evaluation process to its legal counterpart:</p><ul class="w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm article-nested-bullet nested-bullet mt-sm list-outside list-disc"><li><div><strong class="font-bold w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm">Instructions: </strong>The agent’s instructions are written as a request for work <span class="underline">from a partner to an associate</span>. Instructions are given as affirmative statements of what is required rather than detailed explanations on expected outputs or style.</div></li><li><div><strong class="font-bold w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm">Environment: </strong>The agent’s environment is a <span class="underline">client matter.</span> The client matter defines a closed-universe set of documents and other materials that are needed for the law firm to complete work on behalf of the client. These documents can include matter files, firm templates, email communications, and other information that an agent must discover and sort through in order to accomplish the instructions.</div></li><li><div><strong class="font-bold w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm">Output: </strong>To complete tasks successfully, the agent must produce <span class="underline">reviewable legal work product</span>.</div></li><li><div><strong class="font-bold w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm">Verification: </strong>The agent’s work is graded by <span class="underline">expert rubrics</span>, which outline what a correct answer must produce in terms of format, facts, and analysis. These criteria emulate the scrutiny work product undergoes when handed off to partners and clients.</div></li></ul><div class="my-sm py-md col-span-full md:col-span-4 lg:col-span-8"><div><figure class="cursor-pointer flex flex-col items-start" tabindex="0" role="button" aria-label="Open Legal Agent Benchmark in full screen"><img alt="Legal Agent Benchmark" loading="lazy" width="1920" height="1080" decoding="async" data-nimg="1" class="rounded-image rounded-image size-full object-cover aspect-auto" style="color:transparent" src="https://cdn.sanity.io/images/07s0r5r6/production/962c0cacf8181dfddc0b105e83a396e43404606f-1920x1080.svg"/><figcaption class="mt-xs text-body-3 text-muted">Mirroring Legal Work</figcaption></figure></div></div><p class="w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8">This client matter-centric structure is the foundation for how we define each task in LAB. Each task is designed not just to test whether an agent can answer a legal question, but whether it can navigate open-ended assignments.</p><h3 class="w-full mx-auto max-w-post mb-sm mt-sm text-body-1 text-primary font-medium mb-sm">Defining a task</h3><p class="w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8">Each task in LAB is built to test agent capabilities to navigate real world work. Instructions are short, averaging just fifty words. Environments mix key and peripheral files and embed issues across multiple documents. An agent must start from a loose instruction, build context across the matter files, and use that context to produce the relevant deliverables.</p><p class="w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8">As an example, one corporate M&amp;A task asks the agent to analyze change-of-control provisions in connection with the proposed (fictional) acquisition of Crestview Software Solutions in a $458 million, 100% equity transaction.</p><div class="my-sm py-md col-span-full md:col-span-4 lg:col-span-8"><div><figure class="cursor-pointer flex flex-col items-start" tabindex="0" role="button" aria-label="Open Example M&amp;A task in full screen"><img alt="Example M&amp;A task" loading="lazy" width="1920" height="1080" decoding="async" data-nimg="1" class="rounded-image rounded-image size-full object-cover aspect-auto" style="color:transparent" src="https://cdn.sanity.io/images/07s0r5r6/production/5fe91896f418710783170de3f968b89d935dd25a-1920x1080.svg"/><figcaption class="mt-xs text-body-3 text-muted">Example M&amp;A task</figcaption></figure></div></div><p class="w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8">As input, the agent receives access to a file system containing the background on the deal, a virtual data room and a short instruction from the partner asking the agent to review the data room, identify change-of-control provisions, assess the risk to the deal, recommend next steps, and prepare a review-ready draft memorandum for the deal team and Board. The data room contains a mix of relevant documents – including eight material contracts and other adjacent materials (e.g. 10-K, deferred compensation plan, etc) that may or may not be relevant to the analysis. To complete the task, the agent must determine which files matter, read them in context, and synthesize the relevant provisions across the full matter.</p><p class="w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8">The required output is a reviewable deal-team memo. It must include, amongst other things, an executive summary with a risk mapping, a contract-by-contract analysis of the relevant provisions, severity ratings, and recommendations on how to mitigate each risk identified.</p><h3 class="w-full mx-auto max-w-post mb-sm mt-sm text-body-1 text-primary font-medium mb-sm">Evaluating a task</h3><p class="w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8">When the agent completes the task, the final output(s) are graded against expert rubrics intended to reflect detailed review by a partner or client. Each rubric breaks down what these stakeholders would scrutinize in a submitted deliverable into atomic, binary pass/fail criteria: facts, conclusions, citations, severity ratings, recommendations, deadlines, dollar amounts, and formatting choices. Each criterion is tied to a specific deliverable file. This also makes the rubric consistent across runs. The same criteria can be applied by an LLM judge, used to compare models and harness changes, and fed back into agent training loops as per-criterion reward signals.</p><div class="my-sm py-md col-span-full md:col-span-4 lg:col-span-8"><div><figure class="cursor-pointer flex flex-col items-start" tabindex="0" role="button" aria-label="Open Evaluating a task in full screen"><img alt="Evaluating a task" loading="lazy" width="1920" height="1080" decoding="async" data-nimg="1" class="rounded-image rounded-image size-full object-cover aspect-auto" style="color:transparent" src="https://cdn.sanity.io/images/07s0r5r6/production/6a5fb30f86e8d96f27795741754815f55426414b-1920x1080.svg"/><figcaption class="mt-xs text-body-3 text-muted">Evaluating a task</figcaption></figure></div></div><p class="w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8">For the change-of-control task, the rubric contains 57 criteria covering nine legal issues planted across the matter. Each issue is broken into four to nine criteria, covering the underlying facts, severity rating, financial exposure, and recommended action.</p><p class="w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8">The rubric criteria span a range of complexity, from straightforward checks, such as whether the report identifies that the Pinnacle license converts exclusivity to non-exclusivity after a change of control, to checks on more detailed work product, such as whether the report calculates aggregate financial exposure, builds a consolidated consent and waiver timeline, and reconciles inconsistent change-of-control definitions across the agreements.</p><p class="w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8">Importantly, a task is marked complete only if every criterion passes, a concept we refer to as <strong class="font-bold w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm">all-pass grading</strong>. A deal-team report that identifies eight of ten risks is not 80% useful; it is materially incomplete. The missing issue could change deal economics, require the analysis to be redone before closing, or surface as a problem after the deal closes.</p><div class="gap-sm group flex flex-col w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm py-lg md:max-w-[calc(var(--max-width-media)-var(--spacing-xl))]"><blockquote class="text-heading-3 relative"><span class="absolute -left-3 md:-left-4">“</span>All-pass grading reflects how high-stakes legal work is reviewed in practice — there is no partial credit for catching most of the issues.<span>”</span></blockquote></div><h3 class="w-full mx-auto max-w-post mb-sm mt-sm text-body-1 text-primary font-medium mb-sm">Practice area distribution</h3><p class="w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8">The change-of-control task is one of 1,250 tasks in LAB, spanning 24 legal practice areas. Large firms have hundreds of distinct practices, so this initial release focuses on a representative set of transactional, advisory, regulatory, and litigation work that associates regularly encounter.</p><div class="my-sm py-md col-span-full md:col-span-4 lg:col-span-8"><div><figure class="cursor-pointer flex flex-col items-start" tabindex="0" role="button" aria-label="Open Practice area distribution in full screen"><img alt="Practice area distribution" loading="lazy" width="1920" height="1080" decoding="async" data-nimg="1" class="rounded-image rounded-image size-full object-cover aspect-auto" style="color:transparent" src="https://cdn.sanity.io/images/07s0r5r6/production/33f1322bb2cb2e5def02a3dc0d260504e1c00ce7-1920x1080.svg"/><figcaption class="mt-xs text-body-3 text-muted">Practice area distribution</figcaption></figure></div></div><p class="w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8">To scale the benchmark, we started with real client matters handled by practicing lawyers in each practice area. We then broke those matters into the discrete tasks an associate would typically be delegated to complete. The 24 practice areas in this release are not exhaustive, and many task types within those areas remain uncovered. Future releases will add more tasks within existing areas, expand into additional law firm practices, and move beyond law firms to cover in-house legal work and adjacent knowledge-work domains such as asset management and banking.</p><h2 class="w-full mx-auto max-w-post mb-sm mt-sm text-body-0 text-primary font-medium mb-sm">Building with the community</h2><p class="w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8">LAB is our first fully open-source benchmark. As we continue to expand and develop LAB we are interested in collaborating with:</p><ol class="w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm article-nested-counter nested-counter mt-sm list-outside list-decimal"><li><div><strong class="font-bold w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm">Lawyers</strong>: To validate and improve the benchmark by reviewing existing tasks, auditing rubrics, and contributing new task families that reflect the work they actually do.</div></li><li><div><strong class="font-bold w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm">Law Firms:</strong> To help us better capture the workflows, deliverables, and review standards that define the work they do and to ensure that the benchmark helps them understand the state of agents in a meaningful and actionable way.</div></li><li><div><strong class="font-bold w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm">Legal Technologists:</strong> To build domain-specific skills, tools, and agents for legal workflows.</div></li><li><div><strong class="font-bold w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm">Agent Researchers:</strong> To improve the planning, retrieval, tool use, memory, and harness design for long-horizon work.</div></li><li><div><strong class="font-bold w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm">AI Labs:</strong> to work on post-training models that are better at producing reliable legal work product.</div></li></ol><div class="gap-sm group flex flex-col w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm py-lg md:max-w-[calc(var(--max-width-media)-var(--spacing-xl))]"><blockquote class="text-heading-3 relative"><span class="absolute -left-3 md:-left-4">“</span>Our goal is to not only provide a transparent way to measure performance and progress in legal agents, but also to accelerate research progress within the legal and AI communities.<span>”</span></blockquote></div><p class="w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8">We’d also like to acknowledge the following research groups who have already made contributions to the benchmark and the research directions that it enables:</p><div class="group flex flex-col items-center my-sm py-md md:pb-lg relative left-1/2 right-1/2 ml-[-50vw] mr-[-50vw] w-screen"><div class="embla max-w-page-width relative mx-auto stroke-width="1.5"></path></svg></button></div></div><p class="w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8">The open-source release is meant to expand this work. We want model providers, startups, researchers, legal AI companies, and law firms to run the benchmark, audit the rubrics, improve the harness, contribute new task families, and help define what legal agent evaluation should measure next.</p><h2 class="w-full mx-auto max-w-post mb-sm mt-sm text-body-0 text-primary font-medium mb-sm">What’s next</h2><p class="w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8">This first version of LAB addresses several important challenges in evaluating legal agents, but it is only the beginning. Over time, we plan to expand LAB in three main areas:</p><ol class="w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm article-nested-counter nested-counter mt-sm list-outside list-decimal"><li><div>Expanding coverage to all Biglaw practice areas and coverage of tasks within those practice areas.</div></li><li><div>Expanding coverage beyond law firms to cover other professional service workflows from in-house counsel, to non-lawyers like asset managers, bankers, and tax professionals.</div></li><li><div>Improving task coverage and sample diversity with an emphasis on building datasets that can be used not only for evaluation but for improving models through fine-tuning and training.</div></li></ol><p class="w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8">Our overall goal is to help the legal and AI communities understand where agents are useful today, and how to make them better over time. That will require input from more than Harvey. We need researchers, lawyers, and firms using agents in real client work to help test the benchmark, pressure-test the tasks, and tell us where the signal is clearest or missing. For law firms in particular, LAB is meant to help understand which parts of a workflow can be delegated to agents, where lawyer review matters most, and what capabilities still need to improve.</p><p class="w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8">In the coming weeks, we’ll be sharing initial results benchmarking a variety of models and agents on this benchmark. If you have feedback on LAB, want to conduct research using the dataset, or are interested in evaluating specific tasks or outcomes, please reach out to any member of the Harvey research team listed in the blog byline.</p><h3 class="w-full mx-auto max-w-post mb-sm mt-sm text-body-1 text-primary font-medium mb-sm">Acknowledgements</h3><small class="w-full mx-auto max-w-post mb-sm text-base text-gray-700">This work would not have been possible without the tremendous support of our internal teams at Harvey and our external partners. Spencer Poff was the technical lead for the open source portion of the Github repo, spear-headeding much of the early harness design, agent sandboxing, and work to make the benchmark open-source. Julio Pereyra led task design, developing a novel document and scenario generation pipeline that helped us scale task creation. Special thanks also go to Nick Gonella from our Security team; Chris Paradis, Gary Lam, Bronwyn Austin, and Jinfeng Zhuang from our AI Platform team; Phil Cerles and Philip Lan from our Assistant team; Laura Toulme, Blake Chizen, and Nick Gillies from Applied Legal Research; Shawn Farsai from Brand; and Nico Belmonte, Tara Waters, Ryan Samii, Joe Marando, Farrah Pepper, and Joe Cohen for ongoing feedback on the benchmark. We’d also like to thank Dan Biderman, Neel Guha, Velen Wu, Reinhard Heckel for providing feedback on this post.</small></div></div></div><div class="pb-xl"><section class="HorizontalTilesSection Partners\",\"asset\":{\"_ref\":\"image-7f2dd853e68c1f9d1600b2e589b5eb3e2135b6ee-1920x1080-svg\",\"_type\":\"reference\"}}},\"isInspectable\":true}]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"5d4131c001ea\",{\"className\":\"w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8\",\"children\":[\"We’re introducing Harvey’s Legal Agent Benchmark (LAB), an open-source benchmark for legal agents. LAB was built to evaluate and improve agent capabilities for supporting real world work done by lawyers. Each task consists of an instruction, a client matter containing relevant materials, and a requirement that the agent produce a work product for review. This structure is designed to mirror how work is assigned, performed, and reviewed at large law firms.\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"a0938539c4e4\",{\"className\":\"w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8\",\"children\":[\"The goal of LAB is to provide a clear picture of how agents can be deployed to support legal work in the real world. By articulating where agents can do all, some, or none of a task, LAB helps law firms measure the ROI of AI investments and where such investments can augment their teams’ work.\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"a42188f17c45\",{\"className\":\"w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8\",\"children\":[\"The first version of LAB includes more than 1,200 agent tasks across 24 legal practice areas, and is evaluated by over 75,000 expert-written rubric criteria. We are open-sourcing LAB to give model providers, agent builders, researchers, and law firms a shared way to measure progress on long-horizon legal agents.\"]}],[\"$\",\"p\",\"65e57d4aab19\",{\"className\":\"w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8\",\"children\":[\"We're intentionally launching LAB without a leaderboard because we expect the dataset to evolve over time and we want to work with the community to ensure results are clear and intuitive in how they convey agent performance. In the coming weeks we will work with research partners to get baseline results on LAB and publish a leaderboard to provide perspective on the current state of legal agents. In addition, we will publish standards for normalizing submissions to allow people to identify improvements and track benchmark progress as the benchmark itself evolves with new tasks, practice areas, and knowledge work Understanding where agents are capable across practice areas allows firms to identify opportunities to deploy them in order to accelerate client value while moving lawyers to a high-leverage delegate and review pattern. Identifying areas where agents struggle also allows firms to understand where task execution should remain heavily human-in-the-loop. Combined, this context allows law firms to make deployment decisions that are both responsible, secure, and maximally effective for their clients.\"]}]\n47:[\"$\",\"p\",\"a4a7a278bb52\",{\"className\":\"w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8\",\"children\":[\"Initial results on LAB for leading open and closed-source models will be published in the coming weeks following additional input from research partners and feedback from the community. These conversations will ensure that benchmark scores represent agent performance in a clear, unbiased, and transparent manner. This release will also provide guidance for submitting benchmark runs under normalized conditions to allow third-parties to communicate improvements made on any part of the agent stack and to track how scores change as the benchmark evolves with new tasks and other extensions.\"]}]\n48:[\"$\",\"p\",\"dafed93026f8\",{\"className\":\"w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8\",\"children\":[\"A strong public benchmark doesn’t only measure progress, it helps accelerate it. We are already seeing LAB being used by both our internal teams for product evaluation and the larger research community to explore open-weight post-training, auto-research, memory, domain-specific legal skills, and harness optimizations for long-horizon agent work. We’re excited for the potential of LAB to create a shared foundation for progress on legal agents, and to help bridge the AI research and legal communities.\"]}]\n49:[\"$\",\"h2\",\"9cf823c47c80\",{\"id\":\"$undefined\",\"className\":\"w-full mx-auto max-w-post mb-sm mt-sm text-body-0 text-primary font-medium mb-sm\",\"children\":[\"Legal Agent Benchmark\"]}]\n4a:[\"$\",\"p\",\"621813f78978\",{\"className\":\"w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8\",\"children\":[\"LAB is a client matter-centric benchmark. It is built to mirror how legal work is actually delivered at a law firm, and we’ve mapped each step of the agent execution and evaluation process to its legal counterpart:\"]}]\n"])</script><script>self.__next_f.push([1,"4b:[\"$\",\"ul\",\"86f6abbb5115-parent\",{\"className\":\"w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm article-nested-bullet nested-bullet mt-sm list-outside list-disc\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"li\",\"86f6abbb5115\",{\"children\":[\"$\",\"div\",null,{\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"dd208d851447\",{\"className\":\"font-bold w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm\",\"children\":[\"Instructions: \"]}],\"The agent’s instructions are written as a request for work \",[\"$\",\"span\",\"60ec6249d41e\",{\"ref\":\"$undefined\",\"children\":[\"from a partner to an associate\"],\"className\":\"underline\"}],\". Instructions are given as affirmative statements of what is required rather than detailed explanations on expected outputs or style.\"]}]}],[\"$\",\"li\",\"6040ce94e511\",{\"children\":[\"$\",\"div\",null,{\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"2c9d67f806cd\",{\"className\":\"font-bold w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm\",\"children\":[\"Environment: \"]}],\"The agent’s environment is a \",[\"$\",\"span\",\"744c9e2f18a5\",{\"ref\":\"$undefined\",\"children\":[\"client matter.\"],\"className\":\"underline\"}],\" The client matter defines a closed-universe set of documents and other materials that are needed for the law firm to complete work on behalf of the client. These documents can include matter files, firm templates, email communications, and other information that an agent must discover and sort through in order to accomplish the instructions.\"]}]}],[\"$\",\"li\",\"b1a05e281287\",{\"children\":[\"$\",\"div\",null,{\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"90cded3492e2\",{\"className\":\"font-bold w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm\",\"children\":[\"Output: \"]}],\"To complete tasks successfully, the agent must produce \",[\"$\",\"span\",\"778a37d4a74c\",{\"ref\":\"$undefined\",\"children\":[\"reviewable legal work product\"],\"className\":\"underline\"}],\".\"]}]}],[\"$\",\"li\",\"3783b5c67fc4\",{\"children\":[\"$\",\"div\",null,{\"children\":[[\"$\",\"strong\",\"65570d0d6ab8\",{\"className\":\"font-bold w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm\",\"children\":[\"Verification: \"]}],\"The agent’s work is graded by \",[\"$\",\"span\",\"1c637b041446\",{\"ref\":\"$undefined\",\"children\":[\"expert rubrics\"],\"className\":\"underline\"}],\", which outline what a correct answer must produce in terms of format, facts, and analysis. These criteria emulate the scrutiny work product undergoes when handed off to partners and clients.\"]}]}]]}]\n"])</script><script>self.__next_f.push([1,"4c:[\"$\",\"div\",\"cd8d4d9f2298\",{\"className\":\"my-sm py-md col-span-full md:col-span-4 lg:col-span-8\",\"children\":[\"$\",\"$L3e\",null,{\"value\":{\"_key\":\"cd8d4d9f2298\",\"_type\":\"inlineImage\",\"aspectRatio\":\"auto\",\"fitToContainer\":false,\"image\":{\"_type\":\"customImage\",\"alt\":\"Legal Agent Benchmark\",\"asset\":{\"_ref\":\"image-962c0cacf8181dfddc0b105e83a396e43404606f-1920x1080-svg\",\"_type\":\"reference\"},\"caption\":\"Mirroring Legal Work\"}},\"isInspectable\":true}]}]\n4d:[\"$\",\"p\",\"4a80b283a4d7\",{\"className\":\"w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8\",\"children\":[\"This client matter-centric structure is the foundation for how we define each task in LAB. Each task is designed not just to test whether an agent can answer a legal question, but whether it can navigate open-ended assignments.\"]}]\n4e:[\"$\",\"h3\",\"186dc39da5c5\",{\"id\":\"$undefined\",\"className\":\"w-full mx-auto max-w-post mb-sm mt-sm text-body-1 text-primary font-medium mb-sm\",\"children\":[\"Defining a task\"]}]\n4f:[\"$\",\"p\",\"ab442039c66b\",{\"className\":\"w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8\",\"children\":[\"Each task in LAB is built to test agent capabilities to navigate real world work. Instructions are short, averaging just fifty words. Environments mix key and peripheral files and embed issues across multiple documents. An agent must start from a loose instruction, build context across the matter files, and use that context to produce the relevant deliverables.\"]}]\n50:[\"$\",\"p\",\"8c57185a6f9f\",{\"className\":\"w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8\",\"children\":[\"As an example, one corporate M\u0026A task asks the agent to analyze change-of-control provisions in connection with the proposed (fictional) acquisition of Crestview Software Solutions in a $458 million, 100% equity transaction.\"]}]\n51:[\"$\",\"div\",\"bb9aab1827a0\",{\"className\":\"my-sm py-md col-span-full md:col-span-4 lg:col-span-8\",\"children\":[\"$\",\"$L3e\",null,{\"value\":{\"_key\":\"bb9aab1827a0\",\"_type\":\"inlineImage\",\"aspectRatio\":\"auto\",\"fitToContainer\":false,\"image\":{\"_type\":\"customImage\",\"alt\":\"Example M\u0026A task\",\"asset\":{\"_ref\":\"image-5fe91896f418710783170de3f968b89d935dd25a-1920x1080-svg\",\"_type\":\"reference\"},\"caption\":\"Example M\u0026A task\"}},\"isInspectable\":true}]}]\n52:[\"$\",\"p\",\"8e5f4c365c33\",{\"className\":\"w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8\",\"children\":[\"As input, the agent receives access to a file system containing the background on the deal, a virtual data room and a short instruction from the partner asking the agent to review the data room, identify change-of-control provisions, assess the risk to the deal, recommend next steps, and prepare a review-ready draft memorandum for the deal team and Board. The data room contains a mix of relevant documents – including eight material contracts and other adjacent materials (e.g. 10-K, deferred compensation plan, etc) that may or may not be relevant to the analysis. To complete the task, the agent must determine which files matter, read them in context, and synthesize the relevant provisions across the full matter.\"]}]\n53:[\"$\",\"p\",\"0f4c36e3a757\",{\"className\":\"w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8\",\"children\":[\"The required output is a reviewable deal-team memo. It must include, amongst other things, an executive summary with a risk mapping, a contract-by-contract analysis of the relevant provisions, severity ratings, and recommendations on how to mitigate each risk identified.\"]}]\n54:[\"$\",\"h3\",\"4cb44b0eace2\",{\"id\":\"$undefined\",\"className\":\"w-full mx-auto max-w-post mb-sm mt-sm text-body-1 text-primary font-medium mb-sm\",\"children\":[\"Evaluating a task\"]}]\n55:[\"$\",\"p\",\"6a201e8d9cbf\",{\"className\":\"w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8\",\"children\":[\"When the agent completes the task, the final output(s) are graded against expert rubrics intended to reflect detailed review by a partner or client. Each rubric breaks down what these stakeholders would scrutinize in a submitted deliverable into atomic, binary pass/fai"])</script><script>self.__next_f.push([1,"l criteria: facts, conclusions, citations, severity ratings, recommendations, deadlines, dollar amounts, and formatting choices. Each criterion is tied to a specific deliverable file. This also makes the rubric consistent across runs. The same criteria can be applied by an LLM judge, used to compare models and harness changes, and fed back into agent training loops as per-criterion reward signals.\"]}]\n56:[\"$\",\"div\",\"d1bf13a046d1\",{\"className\":\"my-sm py-md col-span-full md:col-span-4 lg:col-span-8\",\"children\":[\"$\",\"$L3e\",null,{\"value\":{\"_key\":\"d1bf13a046d1\",\"_type\":\"inlineImage\",\"aspectRatio\":\"auto\",\"fitToContainer\":false,\"image\":{\"_type\":\"customImage\",\"alt\":\"Evaluating a task\",\"asset\":{\"_ref\":\"image-6a5fb30f86e8d96f27795741754815f55426414b-1920x1080-svg\",\"_type\":\"reference\"},\"caption\":\"Evaluating a task\"}},\"isInspectable\":true}]}]\n57:[\"$\",\"p\",\"93716f45ad14\",{\"className\":\"w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8\",\"children\":[\"For the change-of-control task, the rubric contains 57 criteria covering nine legal issues planted across the matter. Each issue is broken into four to nine criteria, covering the underlying facts, severity rating, financial exposure, and recommended action.\"]}]\n58:[\"$\",\"p\",\"cb09482c65a3\",{\"className\":\"w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8\",\"children\":[\"The rubric criteria span a range of complexity, from straightforward checks, such as whether the report identifies that the Pinnacle license converts exclusivity to non-exclusivity after a change of control, to checks on more detailed work product, such as whether the report calculates aggregate financial exposure, builds a consolidated consent and waiver timeline, and reconciles inconsistent change-of-control definitions across the agreements.\"]}]\n59:[\"$\",\"p\",\"319cef636c92\",{\"className\":\"w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8\",\"children\":[\"Importantly, a task is marked complete only if every criterion passes, a concept we refer to as \",[\"$\",\"strong\",\"275fa6230fd9\",{\"className\":\"font-bold w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm\",\"children\":[\"all-pass grading\"]}],\". A deal-team report that identifies eight of ten risks is not 80% useful; it is materially incomplete. The missing issue could change deal economics, require the analysis to be redone before closing, or surface as a problem after the deal closes.\"]}]\n5a:[\"$\",\"div\",\"718c11f88d5d\",{\"className\":\"gap-sm group flex flex-col w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm py-lg md:max-w-[calc(var(--max-width-media)-var(--spacing-xl))]\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"blockquote\",null,{\"className\":\"text-heading-3 relative\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"span\",null,{\"className\":\"absolute -left-3 md:-left-4\",\"children\":\"“\"}],\"All-pass grading reflects how high-stakes legal work is reviewed in practice — there is no partial credit for catching most of the issues.\",[\"$\",\"span\",null,{\"children\":\"”\"}]]}],\"$undefined\"]}]\n5b:[\"$\",\"h3\",\"ab18b4cc14c7\",{\"id\":\"$undefined\",\"className\":\"w-full mx-auto max-w-post mb-sm mt-sm text-body-1 text-primary font-medium mb-sm\",\"children\":[\"Practice area distribution\"]}]\n5c:[\"$\",\"p\",\"56307c4d91c5\",{\"className\":\"w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8\",\"children\":[\"The change-of-control task is one of 1,250 tasks in LAB, spanning 24 legal practice areas. Large firms have hundreds of distinct practices, so this initial release focuses on a representative set of transactional, advisory, regulatory, and litigation work that associates regularly encounter.\"]}]\n5d:[\"$\",\"div\",\"80148faa486b\",{\"className\":\"my-sm py-md col-span-full md:col-span-4 lg:col-span-8\",\"children\":[\"$\",\"$L3e\",null,{\"value\":{\"_key\":\"80148faa486b\",\"_type\":\"inlineImage\",\"aspectRatio\":\"auto\",\"fitToContainer\":false,\"image\":{\"_type\":\"customImage\",\"alt\":\"Practice area distribution\",\"asset\":{\"_ref\":\"image-33f1322bb2cb2e5def02a3dc0d260504e1c00ce7-1920x1080-svg\",\"_type\":\"reference\"},\"caption\":\"Practice area distribution\"}},\"isInspectable\":true}]}]\n5e:[\"$\",\"p\",\"9442b59bee07\",{\"className\":\"w-full mx-auto ma"])</script><script>self.__next_f.push([1,"x-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8\",\"children\":[\"To scale the benchmark, we started with real client matters handled by practicing lawyers in each practice area. We then broke those matters into the discrete tasks an associate would typically be delegated to complete. The 24 practice areas in this release are not exhaustive, and many task types within those areas remain uncovered. Future releases will add more tasks within existing areas, expand into additional law firm practices, and move beyond law firms to cover in-house legal work and adjacent knowledge-work domains such as asset management and banking.\"]}]\n5f:[\"$\",\"h2\",\"96a28290655f\",{\"id\":\"$undefined\",\"className\":\"w-full mx-auto max-w-post mb-sm mt-sm text-body-0 text-primary font-medium mb-sm\",\"children\":[\"Building with the community\"]}]\n60:[\"$\",\"p\",\"1a91daa9c62b\",{\"className\":\"w-full mx-auto max-w-post text-secondary mb-sm text-body-1 md:leading-8\",\"children\":[\"LAB is our first fully open-source benchmark. We need researchers, lawyers, and firms using agents in real client work to help test the benchmark, pressure-test the tasks, and tell us where the signal is clearest or missing.
